Most days I just lie on the couch in my uniform because everything feels like too much effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any times where you felt like you were hearing voices or seeing things that others could not, and how long exactly have these specific concerns about people plotting against you been the primary focus of your life?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't heard any voices or seen anything unusual like that. The intense feeling that people are plotting against me and changing my routes has really taken over as my main focus for the last couple of months, though it started as a nagging suspicion over the past year.\n\nDoctor: Besides the suspicion about your coworkers, have you noticed a significant decline in your ability to speak fluidly or express emotions, such as your face appearing 'flat' or your speech becoming very limited?\n\nPatient: Yes, I feel completely empty inside and don't really show any emotion anymore. It takes so much effort to speak that I usually just give my husband one-word answers when he tries to talk to me. I've just stopped caring about everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your ability to perform your job duties or manage your daily hygiene has significantly declined over the past year, beyond just the feelings of suspicion?\n\nPatient: Yes, it's gotten really bad over the last year, and I'm actually on a final warning at work now because of the mistakes. At home, I don't even have the energy to shower, cook, or help with chores anymore. Most days I just lie on the couch in my work uniform because doing anything at all feels like too much effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your speech has become incoherent or that you have begun to act in ways that seem bizarre or purposeless to others, such as sudden, odd movements or strange facial expressions?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've had moments where I just freeze at my desk, staring at the screen or holding the phone without answering it. Other times, I pace up and down the hallway muttering about the routes under my breath. I know people are watching me weirdly, but I just can't seem to make myself act normal in those moments.\n\nDoctor: During those times when you freeze or pace, do you feel like you are unable to move or speak even when you want to, or is it more that your thoughts feel jumbled and your actions feel disorganized or unpredictable?\n\nPatient: It feels more like my thoughts are completely jumbled and I just can't force myself to act normal. When I freeze at my desk or pace the hallways muttering about the routes, I know people are staring at me, but I can't make myself stop. It's like my mind and my actions are totally disconnected in those moments and I just can't behave the way I'm supposed to."

Other times, I pace up and down the hallway muttering about the routes under my breath. I know people are watching me weirdly, but I just can't seem to make myself act normal in those moments.\n\nDoctor: During those times when you freeze or pace, do you feel like you are unable to move or speak even when you want to, or is it more that your thoughts feel jumbled and your actions feel disorganized or unpredictable?\n\nPatient: It feels more like my thoughts are completely jumbled and I just can't force myself to act normal. When I freeze at my desk or pace the hallways muttering about the routes, I know people are staring at me, but I can't make myself stop. It's like my mind and my actions are totally disconnected in those moments and I just can't behave the way I'm supposed to.\n\nDoctor: When you freeze at your desk, do you ever find yourself holding a fixed, unusual position for a long period, or do you find that your behaviors are primarily characterized by disorganized, purposeless movements?\n\nPatient: I don't think I hold strange positions, but I do just freeze up and stare at the screen or hold the phone without answering it. It's more that I get stuck pacing the hallway and muttering the routes under my breath, and I just can't force myself to act normally during those times."

Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.
